=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an integer array nums of length n and a 2D array queries, where queries[i] = [l_i, r_i, val_i].
Each queries[i] represents the following action on nums:

Select a subset of indices in the range [l_i, r_i] from nums.
Decrement the value at each selected index by exactly val_i.

A Zero Array is an array with all its elements equal to 0.
Return the minimum possible non-negative value of k, such that after processing the first k queries in sequence, nums becomes a Zero Array. If no such k exists, return -1.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,0,2], queries = [[0,2,1],[0,2,1],[1,1,3]]
Output: 2
Explanation:

For query 0 (l = 0, r = 2, val = 1):

Decrement the values at indices [0, 2] by 1.
The array will become [1, 0, 1].


For query 1 (l = 0, r = 2, val = 1):

Decrement the values at indices [0, 2] by 1.
The array will become [0, 0, 0], which is a Zero Array. Therefore, the minimum value of k is 2.




Example 2:

Input: nums = [4,3,2,1], queries = [[1,3,2],[0,2,1]]
Output: -1
Explanation:
It is impossible to make nums a Zero Array even after all the queries.

Example 3:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,2,1], queries = [[0,1,1],[1,2,1],[2,3,2],[3,4,1],[4,4,1]]
Output: 4
Explanation:

For query 0 (l = 0, r = 1, val = 1):

Decrement the values at indices [0, 1] by 1.
The array will become [0, 1, 3, 2, 1].


For query 1 (l = 1, r = 2, val = 1):

Decrement the values at indices [1, 2] by 1.
The array will become [0, 0, 2, 2, 1].


For query 2 (l = 2, r = 3, val = 2):

Decrement the values at indices [2, 3] by 2.
The array will become [0, 0, 0, 0, 1].


For query 3 (l = 3, r = 4, val = 1):

Decrement the value at index 4 by 1.
The array will become [0, 0, 0, 0, 0]. Therefore, the minimum value of k is 4.




Example 4:

Input: nums = [1,2,3,2,6], queries = [[0,1,1],[0,2,1],[1,4,2],[4,4,4],[3,4,1],[4,4,5]]
Output: 4

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10
0 <= nums[i] <= 1000
1 <= queries.length <= 1000
queries[i] = [l_i, r_i, val_i]
0 <= l_i <= r_i < nums.length
1 <= val_i <= 10

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        q = len(queries)

        def can(k: int) -> bool:
            for i, target in enumerate(nums):
                if target == 0:
                    continue
                mask = (1 << (target + 1)) - 1
                bits = 1
                for j in range(k):
                    l, r, v = queries[j]
                    if l <= i <= r:
                        bits = (bits | (bits << v)) & mask
                        # optional early if bit target set? But further queries could not unset; if target set, can break? Since adding more queries cannot hurt; if target reached, for this index done. But if break, okay because we only need existence. But if we break early, no need process rest. However, if target reached, it remains reachable with more queries (ignore rest). So can break. But careful: if break, we don't update bits, but we only need final bit. It remains true. So can break to optimize. But not necessary. Could add if (bits >> target) & 1: break. But if break, then for this index okay. Simpler no break.
                if ((bits >> target) & 1) == 0:
                    return False
            return True

        if can(0):
            return 0
        if not can(q):
            return -1

        lo, hi = 0, q
        while lo < hi:
            mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            if can(mid):
                hi = mid
            else:
                lo = mid + 1
        return lo

class Solution:
    def minZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        bits = [1] * n
        masks = [(1 << (x + 1)) - 1 if x > 0 else 1 for x in nums]? Actually if x=0 mask=1. But if x=0, bits stays1. Could compute mask for each index.
        if all(((bits[i] >> nums[i]) & 1) for i in range(n)): return 0
        for idx, (l,r,v) in enumerate(queries, 1):
            for i in range(l, r+1):
                target = nums[i]
                if target == 0:
                    continue
                bits[i] = (bits[i] | (bits[i] << v)) & ((1 << (target + 1)) - 1)
            if all(((bits[i] >> nums[i]) & 1) for i in range(n)):
                return idx
        return -1

Let's ensure code is syntactically correct:
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        bits = [1] * n
        masks = [(1 << (x + 1)) - 1 for x in nums]
        done = [x == 0 for x in nums]
        remaining = n - sum(done)

        if remaining == 0:
            return 0

        for idx, (l, r, v) in enumerate(queries, 1):
            for i in range(l, r + 1):
                if done[i]:
                    continue
                bits[i] = (bits[i] | (bits[i] << v)) & masks[i]
                if (bits[i] >> nums[i]) & 1:
                    done[i] = True
                    remaining -= 1
            if remaining == 0:
                return idx

        return -1

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
to the user. Need output final code only. We can final. But let's ensure no issue with "remaining" and "done" if target=0: done true. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with "masks" if nums[i]=0: (1 << 1) -1 =1. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with "if (bits[i] >> nums[i]) & 1:" if nums[i]=0 and done false? done true for zero, so skip. If somehow done false for zero, bits>>0 &1 true, would mark done. But done initial true. Good.

Now, final code.</think>```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minZeroArray(self, nums: List[int], queries: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        bits = [1] * n
        masks = [(1 << (x + 1)) - 1 for x in nums]
        done = [x == 0 for x in nums]
        remaining = n - sum(done)

        if remaining == 0:
            return 0

        for idx, (l, r, v) in enumerate(queries, 1):
            for i in range(l, r + 1):
                if done[i]:
                    continue
                bits[i] = (bits[i] | (bits[i] << v)) & masks[i]
                if (bits[i] >> nums[i]) & 1:
                    done[i] = True
                    remaining -= 1

            if remaining == 0:
                return idx

        return -1
```
